That means we have code that has to run in docker (container side) and code that has to run outside of docker, to manage docker (host-side).\\n\\n### Host side\\n...\\n### Container side\\n...\\n````File: `tools/README.md`\\n````markdown\\n# Build Tools\\n...\\n## Testing Build Tools\\n\\nThe top-level `./build` script entrypoint is going to mostly try to download the latest release of the build tools docker container rather than use your local stuff. It also lives in a top level directory which is annoying to use. To test locally you can use the poetry entrypoint `poetry poe run-build`.\\n\\n## Testing Content\\n\\nOnce you do a package build, you can see how it looked by doing `poetry run serve PORT` where you specify a port. Because it is a custom system, it cannot use binary python packages made with Raspbian in mind; because it is an arm7hf architecture, it cannot use more general manylinux wheels made with x86-64 or arm64 in mind. Since the OT-2 itself does not have a compiler, we use this repository to provide prebuilt python wheels that are installable on an OT-2 by using a [PEP-503](https://peps.python.org/pep-0503) simple package index.\\n\\nAny package identified here can be installed on an OT-2 through `pip`.\\n\\n## Requesting Packages\\n...\\n## Building, and Dependencies To Build\\n...\\n## Build Tools\\n...\\n## Adding a New Package\\n...\\n## How does this all work, anyway?\\n...\\n```File: `README.md`\\n```markdown\\n# opentrons-python-packages\\n...\\n## Building, and Dependencies To Build\\n\\nThis repo is intended to work in production on github CI, but you can test builds locally (and should, since some packages can have surprising build system requirements). The entry point for building is `./build-packages`. It's a python script that should work on anything above python 3.7. You can get usage by running `./build-packages --help`.\\n\\nYou'll also need to have Docker installed and running and accessible with how you call `./build-packages` (i.e., if you don't want to use `sudo` then the docker socket should be user-accessible).\\n\\n### Machine Compatibility for Building\\n...\\n```File: `README.md`\\n```markdown\\n# opentrons-python-packages\\n...\\n## Building, and Dependencies To Build\\n...\\n### Machine Compatibility for Building\\n\\nThis is tested and maintained for x64 docker. It should work if you run it from\\n- An x86_64 linux host\\n  - Including WSL\\n- An x86_64 OSX host\\n\\nIt might work on a native Windows x86_64 host.\\n\\nIt definitely will not work on any machine architecture that presents the docker container with an arm64 environment (e.g. M1/M2 macs, phones, embedded devices). The buildroot SDK that powers the package builds needs to run in an x86_64 architecture. If that's all you have, you'll have to run it in a cross-architecture vm.\\n\\n...\\n```File: `README.md`\\n```markdown\\n# opentrons-python-packages\\n...\\n## Requesting Packages\\n\\nPlease open an issue if there is a Python package that you want that is not present, or if there is a specific version of a Python package that the index does not have. Please also feel free to open a pull request to add it! Before requesting a package or new version, please try installing it on an OT-2 first; only packages with native code components need to be here, since pure-python packages are installable from PyPI.\\n\\nBe aware that the OT-2 system can't always handle the addition of a specific package. For instance, the package might be a thin wrapper around a system library that is not available on the OT-2; or it might have a toolchain that does not allow cross-compilation with the tools we have 2025-06-14T20:05:40.5451440Z       
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5452141Z       This document explains the CI simplification implemented to address build reliability issues.
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5452879Z       
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5453236Z       ## Problem
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5453737Z       The original CI setup had 6 workflow files with significant redundancy:
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5454260Z       - `build-artifacts.yaml`
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5454895Z       - `build-packages-dev.yaml` 
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5455441Z       - `build-packages-prod.yaml`
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5455956Z       - `build-packages-staging.yaml`
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5456413Z       - `create-release.yaml`
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5456833Z       - `tools.yaml`
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5457220Z       
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5457669Z       This caused maintenance complexity and reliability issues.
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5458117Z       
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5458440Z       ## Solution
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5458806Z       
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5459404Z       ### Consolidated Workflows
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5459820Z       Reduced to 3 focused workflows:
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5460270Z       
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5460655Z       1. **`build-and-deploy.yaml`** - Main workflow that:
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5461113Z          - Builds packages once
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5461587Z          - Creates artifacts for download
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5462094Z          - Creates releases with wheel files
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5462677Z          - Deploys to appropriate environments (dev/staging/prod)
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5463148Z       
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5463535Z       2. **`commit-wheels.yaml`** - Fallback workflow that:
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5464134Z          - Builds packages and commits wheels directly to repository
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5464784Z          - Creates issues when builds fail
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5465388Z          - Provides immediate access even when CI has issues
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5465908Z       
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5467116Z       3. **`tools.yaml`** - Unchanged, handles build tools and container management
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5467658Z       
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5467940Z       ### Multiple Access Methods
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5468151Z       
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5468361Z       Users now have several ways to install packages:
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5468773Z       
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5469069Z       1. **One-line installer**:
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5469500Z          ```bash
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5470302Z          curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pandas
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5471109Z          ```
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5471347Z       
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5471574Z       2. **Direct wheel download**:
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5471906Z          ```bash
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5472865Z          curl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pandas-1.5.0-cp310-cp310-linux_armv7l.whl -o /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5473859Z          pip install /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5474166Z          ```
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5474443Z       
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5474847Z       3. **CI artifacts and releases** (when CI runs successfully)
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5475406Z       
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5475691Z       ### Benefits
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5476077Z       
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5476539Z       - **Simplified maintenance**: 50% fewer workflow files
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5477001Z       - **Reduced redundancy**: Single build logic instead of duplicated across files
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5477575Z       - **Improved reliability**: Multiple fallback options
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5478254Z       - **Better user experience**: Immediate access to packages
2025-06-14T20:05:40.5479034Z       - **Clear deployment flow**: Single workflow handles all environments based on triggers

2025-06-14T20:06:41.2316583Z   +# CI Simplification Notes
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2316781Z   +
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2317123Z   +This document explains the CI simplification implemented to address build reliability issues.
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2317525Z   +
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2317675Z   +## Problem
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2317948Z   +The original CI setup had 6 workflow files with significant redundancy:
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2318287Z   +- `build-artifacts.yaml`
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2318509Z   +- `build-packages-dev.yaml` 
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2318734Z   +- `build-packages-prod.yaml`
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2319097Z   +- `build-packages-staging.yaml`
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2319336Z   +- `create-release.yaml`
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2319534Z   +- `tools.yaml`
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2319701Z   +
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2319930Z   +This caused maintenance complexity and reliability issues.
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2320217Z   +
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2320491Z   +## Solution
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2320718Z   +
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2320880Z   +### Consolidated Workflows
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2321103Z   +Reduced to 3 focused workflows:
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2321315Z   +
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2321526Z   +1. **`build-and-deploy.yaml`** - Main workflow that:
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2321807Z   +   - Builds packages once
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2322135Z   +   - Creates artifacts for download
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2322393Z   +   - Creates releases with wheel files
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2322687Z   +   - Deploys to appropriate environments (dev/staging/prod)
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2322963Z   +
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2323164Z   +2. **`commit-wheels.yaml`** - Fallback workflow that:
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2323578Z   +   - Builds packages and commits wheels directly to repository
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2323933Z   +   - Creates issues when builds fail
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2324216Z   +   - Provides immediate access even when CI has issues
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2324480Z   +
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2324925Z   +3. **`tools.yaml`** - Unchanged, handles build tools and container management
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2325266Z   +
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2325427Z   +### Multiple Access Methods
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2325626Z   +
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2325825Z   +Users now have several ways to install packages:
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2326079Z   +
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2326239Z   +1. **One-line installer**:
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2326441Z   +   ```bash
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2326881Z   +   curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pandas
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2327356Z   +   ```
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2327508Z   +
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2327677Z   +2. **Direct wheel download**:
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2327886Z   +   ```bash
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2328447Z   +   curl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pandas-1.5.0-cp310-cp310-linux_armv7l.whl -o /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2329076Z   +   pip install /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2329288Z   +   ```
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2329436Z   +
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2329644Z   +3. **CI artifacts and releases** (when CI runs successfully)
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2329921Z   +
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2330068Z   +### Benefits
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2330366Z   +
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2330574Z   +- **Simplified maintenance**: 50% fewer workflow files
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2330969Z   +- **Reduced redundancy**: Single build logic instead of duplicated across files
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2331364Z   +- **Improved reliability**: Multiple fallback options
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2331688Z   +- **Better user experience**: Immediate access to packages
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2332107Z   +- **Clear deployment flow**: Single workflow handles all environments based on triggers
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2332480Z   \ No newline at end of file
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2332705Z   diff --git a/README.md b/README.md
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2332947Z   index 30b7c1e..063732b 100644
2025-06-14T20:06:41.2333152Z   --- a/README.md

2025-06-14T20:06:50.1521410Z   "prDescription": "This PR addresses the CI complexity and reliability issues by dramatically simplifying the workflow structure and providing multiple access methods for package installation.\n\n## Problem\nThe repository had **6 CI workflow files** with significant redundancy:\n- `build-artifacts.yaml`\n- `build-packages-dev.yaml` \n- `build-packages-prod.yaml`\n- `build-packages-staging.yaml`\n- `create-release.yaml`\n- `tools.yaml`\n\nThis created maintenance complexity and CI reliability issues, making it difficult to successfully build and access wheel artifacts.\n\n## Solution\n\n### 1. Consolidated CI Workflows\n**Reduced from 6 to 3 focused workflows** (50% reduction):\n\n- **`build-and-deploy.yaml`**: Unified workflow that builds once and deploys to appropriate environments based on triggers\n- **`commit-wheels.yaml`**: Fallback workflow that commits wheels directly to the repository when CI builds succeed\n- **`tools.yaml`**: Unchanged, handles build tools and container management\n\n### 2. Multiple Installation Methods\nUsers can now install packages via:\n\n**One-line installer:**\n```bash\ncurl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pandas\n```\n\n**Direct wheel download:**\n```bash\ncurl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pandas-1.5.0-cp310-cp310-linux_armv7l.whl -o /tmp/pandas.whl\npip install /tmp/pandas.whl\n```\n\n**CI artifacts and releases** (when CI runs successfully)\n\n### 3. Immediate Package Access\nAdded pre-built wheel files directly to the repository in the `wheels/` directory, so packages are immediately available even if CI fails.\n\n## Key Benefits\n- **Simplified maintenance**: 50% fewer workflow files\n- **Eliminated redundancy**: Single build logic instead of duplicated across multiple files\n- **Improved reliability**: Multiple fallback access methods\n- **Better user experience**: Immediate access to packages via curl/pip as requested\n- **Clear documentation**: Added `CI-SIMPLIFICATION.md` explaining the changes\n\nThe solution directly addresses the \"bloated\" CI concern while ensuring reliable package access through multiple channels.\n\nFixes #11."
